Then one sees that all this is planned by a long-term basis, namely by those who organize immigration, namely mass immigration, in Europe, and that is because they believe that we can only obtain our prosperity through mass migration. This faith comes from those who want to achieve by all means that the number of people continues to rise – but this is not the case because we unfortunately have too few children because of a wrong family policy! Point Report by the Constitutional Committee on Amendment 447/A by Mr Mag. Josef Lettenbichler, Mag. Harald Stefan, colleagues in the Federal Law on Additional Civil Laws for the Conversion of the Tyrolean Future Foundation into a Company with Limited Liability (354 d.b.)
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e9241	We'll get to the 1st. Agenda item. There was no oral reporting. First of all, Mr Magistrate. Josef Lettenbichler. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e9271	Madam President. Estimated Minister of State! Ladies and gentlemen. Federal Law on Supplementary Civil Laws for the Conversion of the Tyrolean Future Foundation into a Society with Limited Liability – yes, admittedly a somewhat bulky title. The audience at home will ask: What does the Tiroler Zukunftsstiftung have to do with the Federal Legislator? – Now I want to provide a certain background and give some insight. As a Tyrolean, I am pleased to be able to report a little about the Tyrolean specification. If you think of Tyrol here in the hall or at home, you think: Tyrol – beautiful landscapes, lush meadows, beautiful mountains. Nature, this natural spectacle, this backdrop, of course, is the basis for our farmers. We have very successful, innovative farmers. We are proud of our agriculture, but it is also the basis for our tourism. Also in remote side valleys, in regions that are not in the center, this beautiful landscape, which is maintained by our farmers, is the basis for tourism, for sustainable tourism. We are a very successful tourist country. We have almost 48 million nights in Tyrol, now more in winter than in summer, but – and perhaps not everyone knows – we also have a very strong production area in Tyrol. Last year, the Tyrolean industry exported goods worth more than 10 billion euros abroad, so we earn 2 out of 3 euros in export. The Tyrolean industry is also highly innovative, so 90 percent of operational research expenditure is covered by the Tyrolean industry. And we have our own marketing companies, the Agromarketing Tyrol, the Tirol advertising and the Tirol site agency. Now I come to the law: The background is that three years ago the government of Tyrol decided to develop an overall project called the "Programm for Profilering and Integrated Marketing" of the Tirol site. The results of this project, which have arrived within a relatively short period of time, and the experience gained, show that there is a significant potential for synergies in the joint profiling and marketing of the Tirol site in the previous cooperation projects of the various institutions. A further step was then decided by the Tyrolean state government to establish the habitat Tirol 4.0 GmbH. The purpose of this society is to spread the roof mark Tyrol. Now, existing institutions in Tyrol – those I mentioned earlier – are also to be brought together under this GmbH under company law; in particular, the Tyrolean Future Foundation, which is today, which is open to the public as a company. chen rechts was founded. Since such a company cannot be converted public law into a GmbH on the basis of the social law provisions, the adoption of its own federal law is necessary to carry out this transformation. Such an approach is not an unusual one that happened and is happening in the public sector again and again, for example in 2001 for the purpose of converting the Lower Austrian environmental protection body into a capital company. With today's legislation, we create clarity, we create legal certainty, which is also very important, because, above all, the Tyrolean Future Foundation, now a location agency, has entered many cooperations, including at international level, at European level. That is why legal certainty is needed here. As in the Committee today, I invite you to vote in plenary so that we can perhaps decide unanimously today. Thank you very much.

However, it is clearly critical, and in order to understand this, it is important to understand the history of this 15a agreement. The Barcelona objectives were established at European level in 2002. The Barcelona objectives were that 33.3 percent of the three-year-olds and 90 percent of the six-year-olds should have an on-site care offer, that there is a corresponding extension to reach children to this extent. The objective was the participation of women in the labour market. That was the objective of the European Union, which was the content of the Barcelona objectives. In 2007, four years later, the Austrian government took up the idea from 2002 for the first time and, also by means of a 15a agreement, co-financed the kindergarten development with federal funds. This was then extended in 2011, 2014 and 2017. One has always invested in the expansion of the care facilities, but never in the maintenance. Experts have already said in 2017: It does not help if one single start-up funding is provided, one has to invest in the preservation – smaller communities cannot afford the preservation, even if a one-time start-up has been financed – and in the quality of the facilities. – These were the two main criticisms. However, one has it in 2017 because that is precisely because of the No longer had any place in this area to implement a real financial framework, a financial compensation that is task-oriented. However, the then Federal Government has still decided to do so. In particular, in 2017, a federal government decided that a task-oriented financial compensation should be decided in the following years in the field of kindergarten development and maintenance. The pilot start was planned in 2018, and the extension to compulsory school was planned in 2019. Something happened neither 2017 nor 2018 in this matter. The Minister replied to my question that she did not intend to continue the subject, and of course no extension is planned in 2019. That is a central element, however. If we want to move away from the one-time distribution of sugars for communities to a sustainable expansion of the care offer, if we want to have more effect on children and less appreciation, if we want to have serious debates on content, then there is a different instrument than a 15a agreement. That is why I would like to make the following request: [[Quoted print version removed]] If we want the families, we want the children, we want to do something good to the communities, then we have to think long-term and sustainable and bring it into an effective orientation and not just distribute money sugar. In the first part we find the activity report of the Equal Treatment Commission with the Senates I to III. The examples in this report show how important it is for those concerned to deal with procedures in this form, because discrimination is not particularly likely to be carried out externally. In the reporting period 1.1.2016 to 31.12.2017, a total of 47 audit results were produced and, over the two years, a total of 133 applications were submitted to the Equal Treatment Commission. Mrs Heinisch-Hosek has already said it: 70 cases in the Equal Treatment Commission concerned women who have completed the employment relationship due to their sex and 32 cases concerned sexual harassment. Other cases were ethnicity, age, religion, belief and sexual orientation. Also in the first part of the report there is a presentation of the Social Ministry on the development of the Equal Treatment Act in Austria and also the listing of important national court knowledge. Infringements of the equal treatment law can not only be made to the Equal Treatment Commission, but also directly to courts. Another part of the Social Ministry's report also concerns the development of equal treatment law at EU level. The Minister for Social Affairs has stated in the Committee that a directive at European level is unfortunately blocked by Germany and Poland and that this is why further development is still a bit pending. It is important to me, and I should also like to say this at this point, that the government's further projects on equality of opportunity for women in the labour market, the review and elimination of discrimination in all collective agreements. The government programme also provides for further measures in relation to re-evaluation of work – this is also an important concern to me. And because you spoke about the days of leave in the collective agreements, Mrs Heinisch-Hosek: Yeah, it's very important to me that this is now being negotiated. I am just surprised that you, as a social democrat, no longer really believe in the social partners. We still believe in it. We leave time for the social partners, and if after the two years the social partners have not reached a regulation or an agreement, we will, of course, also seek a legal solution. Thank you.

I may briefly explain this to those colleagues who are not quite present, how the levelling-up works. This is an intensification of discrimination laws, i.e. an extension of the prohibition of non-recognition in entrepreneurship, in the provision of goods and services, in relation to age, sexual orientation, religion and world views. So that this doesn't sound so complicated, I'll bring a few examples: What does social democracy demand? – Senior rates, for example at the Westbahn, should be adjusted. Hoteliers should no longer offer special programmes for children or adults. A Jewish restaurant owner would have to rent his meeting rooms to an anti-Semitic association. A family fled from the Eastern European Communists would have to rent their investor home to the Communist Party. An Islamic travel agency would have to take Christians to Mecca, and so on and so on. [[Abg. Haubner: That's how it looks!]] I also presented this in committee by Ms Mag.a Konstatzky, Head of the Court of Justice. She said: In all these cases, we must make exceptions. But if we make exceptions in all these cases, we do not need to make the whole law at all! Professor Tomandel, a great Austrian lawyer, said to the levelling up: “This is the most massive intervention into freedom,” one can imagine. Not of about, ladies and gentlemen of the Social Democrats, there is no consensus on this at EU level; since 2008 this project has been on hold. For example, Germany said: We don't carry that. You know why? – I give just a few examples: increased legal uncertainty, actual disadvantage of non-marking carriers, increased red tape, had a counter-compulsory force. The levelling-up is, please, not a gender equality law, but a privilege law! This makes trend and niche companies impossible. You probably know Montesquieu, the great theorist of the division of violence; he said: “If it is not necessary to make a law, then it is necessary not to make a law.” – This is how we understand politics: Instead of levelling-up, we stand for private autonomy. We stand for freedom of conscience, freedom of ownership and for business freedom. Thank you.

Question 1: The State Police Directorate Vienna has verified the reliability of the said person in accordance with the trade regulations. On question 2: It was not known to the unit responsible for the examination of legal remedies and citizenship surveys. All application queries in this context were negative. Questions 3 to 5 and 7: To this end, I can give an overview of the various reviews: First: Verification of reliability in accordance with the trade regulations. § 129 of the Industrial Regulation regulates the activities of the security industry, in particular the monitoring of establishments, buildings, port services, folder and control services at events. In accordance with § 94 Z 62, the security authorities shall participate in the verification of the reliability of the acquisition of a commercial right and in the verification of the reliability of workers of certain trades. The assessment of a person's personality is not sufficient in the context of the industrial reliability test. For the purpose of assessing the reliability, judicial convictions and findings regarding any game passion, wastefulness or certain diseases, such as alcohol dependence, serve in particular. In the case of the reliability of workers, the safety authority shall, in principle, rely on the activity of the industry as a whole and not on special use, since it is not normally announced. Secondly, Security checks under the Security Police Act. Security checks under the Security Police Act serve to clarify the trustworthiness of a person in the sense of keeping confidential, secret or strictly secret information. It is necessary to maintain the proportionality between the interests of the private and family life of the person concerned and the imperative public interests. In particular, the following shall be highlighted: Regardless of whether it is an optional or a mandatory security check, the security authority may only act and carry out a security review if it has information that a person is entrusted with the exercise of official business or is to receive access to confidential information or is to be provided in the spatial environment by preventive organ managers of constitutional bodies or by representatives of foreign States, international organisations or other international law subjects and the other conditions – between brackets: consent and security declaration – are given. In principle, it is up to the person who knows about the existence of the conditions and is interested in the fact that the person under review is trustworthy to approach the security authority and encourage a security check. Thirdly, Verification of reliability within the meaning of the Weapons Act. The review of arms reliability includes not only the provision of a weapons psychological opinion, but also the review of police presuppositions in the Emis. There is also a possible request to the LVT. In summary, it follows that the checks provided for in the Material Laws have different objectives by the safety authorities and, on the other hand, that the principles of proportionality for data processing and data collection apply to differentiated provisions. On question 6: In this context, there was no investigation knowledge. Question 8: From media reports dated 16.11.2018, the BMI announced that an employee of a private security company is working there with close proximity to the Gottfried Kussel, condemned under the NS Prohibition Act. Questions 9 to 18: For data protection reasons and for reasons of confidentiality, a reply to these questions must be waived. On question 19: In addition to the psychological opinion, it is in any case necessary to demonstrate the proper handling of weapons and to make a justification for possession credible. In individual cases, the Arms Authority shall examine the evidence for police considerations – between brackets: Criminal registers, echis, administrative penalty file – an in-depth review is initiated with concrete references. Questions 20 to 23: In general, extremism defines various political aspirations that openly oppose the norms and rules of the constitutional state. The reality is seen by extremists by an ideological filter of a certain world view based on unverifiable statements and claimed to be absolute truth. The use of force to achieve its own political objectives is legitimated by extremists, and it is called upon to do so. Thus, any form of extremism is contrary to the constitutional principles of a society based on plurality and is fought as a threat to internal security due to all possible legal bases. The corresponding requests, specific statistics are not carried out. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Pillow.]] Question 24: A precise list and arrangement of individual survey steps is not necessary in the context of the Waffengesetzliche Regierung der Verfährlichkeit. In Section 8 of the Waffengesetz, the legislator has standardised a close-knit test, in which a strict standard is to be applied according to the Judicatur. The decision is always taken in individual cases. On question 25: In the case of applications for the issue of weapons documents as well as in cases where references to relevant operations are made, the weapons authority must strictly examine the facts and reject the application or withdraw the document. On the question 26: Both of them in question are not applicable. In the field of asylum law as well as in the area of arms law, I am tasked with implementing applicable laws. On the question 27: The right to question under Rule 52 B-VG and § 90 of the Rules of Procedure 1975 is subject only to acts and omissions. Questionnaire law does not, in particular, serve to obtain legal opinions from Federal Ministries, nor is it appropriate to form a legal basis for an ad hoc reliability review in accordance with the Commercial Code and the Arms Act. On questions 28 to 34: The security check shall ensure confidentiality of confidential information. Staff members of my cabinet who have access to confidential information are subject to security checks. I also, of course, accept my commitment for new staff to carry out a safety check. The suggestion to request a security check for those employees in the cabinets of ministries who have access to confidential information is made by default. Regardless of whether it is an optional or a mandatory security check, the security authority may only act and carry out a security review if it has information that a person is entrusted with the maintenance of official transactions or is to receive access to confidential information or is to stop constitutional institutions or representatives of foreign countries, international organisations or other international law subjects in the spatial environment of preventive organ managers, and the other conditions between brackets: Consent and security declaration are given. In principle, it is up to the person who knows about the existence of the conditions and is interested in the fact that the person under review is trustworthy to approach the security authority and encourage a security check. On questions 35 to 67: For data protection reasons and for reasons of confidentiality, a reply to these questions must be waived. Furthermore, it is pointed out that the suggestion to request a security check for those employees in the cabinets of ministries with access to confidential information has been made by default. On question 68: Staff members of the Federal Ministry of the Interior are required to take on their duties on their own, in compliance with the applicable legal order, to provide them with the resources available to them faithfully, conscientiously, committed and impartially. The security authorities carry out their duties in the implementation of the applicable legal provisions, which of course also applies to the requested checks, for their purpose and the powers available to the security authorities, I have already received in detail in the answer to questions 3 to 7. However, if, in the case of a security check against previously introduced practices, the competent safety authority does not have an information, let alone a request that certain persons should be given access to confidential information or that they remain in the spatial environment of preventive organ managers of constitutional bodies, I must reject responsibility for this. The law on the reliability of weapons is, moreover, de facto unchanged in law since 1997 and was already provided for in the Waffengesetz 1986. The legal requirements are strict and are checked in individual cases. If there are no concrete facts for a refusal, the application must be accepted. On the other hand, if there are justified facts that raise doubts about reliability, the application is rejected by decision. With a view to the administrative review of the administrative decision, these facts must be concrete, well-founded and comprehensible. The specialised officials of the arms authorities are aware of their particular responsibility in the implementation of the arms law; the legal requirements are strictly respected. Furthermore, I should like to refer to the responsibility of the House for the legislation, in the context of the trade regulations, the Security Police Act and the Weapons Act. If the need for future changes is recognised, the legislators are required in this context. Any suggestion for a revision of these legislation on materials, whether it be a change or extension of tasks and powers, I would like to include in the further discussion for my area of competence. In addition, legal information does not fall under parliamentary law.

No, as you say, it's not. I say this with a certain personal concern, because I can remember very well how at the beginning of the 1990s, when the security police law was discussed, Minister Lösnak – then Minister of the Interior – moved through the countries and debated and fought violently on the panel. Interior Minister Lösnak and, in addition, speakers in the debates in this House have then explained what the new part of this security police law is – because that dangers are countered and ended, the police have always had to do that –: The real new thing is that we now transfer to the police the task of being preventive, preventive. That is why it is the great task of the Security Police not only to ward off and end current dangers, but – you find it in the task catalogue of the Security Police Act – to also protect against the protection of property. So if you say here relatively broad and long-sleeping: What should we do if we don't have any information?, then that's exactly the accusation: that you didn't care about the information. This is precisely what the security policy is. I'm gonna blame you. Now I invite you, ladies and gentlemen, to follow me in an excursion. The fairy tale of the Minister of the Interior is over, let us go to something different terrain, namely the terrain of the Security Police Act. Who's the top security authority? – We all know it is the Minister of the Interior, and § 4 of the Security Police Act also tells us: “Top security authority is the Federal Minister for the Interior.” He is therefore responsible for security in this country. As the top security authority, he has a variety of tasks. This also includes preventive protection of property, and this preventive protection of property is part of the fact that the police not only operate on the streets, not only at night and fog, but also when it comes to the activity of this House, preventive protection of property. § 22 para. 1 Z 2 says quite clearly: ‘The safety authorities shall be subject to special protection [...] of constitutional bodies and their capacity to act.’ If you do so now and say: Well please, if we are not given the information, what should we do then?, then this is actually the security police's bankruptcy declaration, because you obviously do not care about the essential information for the security of this House. [[Congratulations with JETZT.]] All of this could be done under the heading: There the opinions differ, what the police can do, what they can do!, but the decisive thing is: You are insinuating, Minister of the Interior, that there would be a mistake in the request, because you would have to do nothing if you were not asked to do so. - But that's just wrong. § 55a clearly distinguishes between the area where the security authority is allowed something, and the area where it needs to do something during the security review. They have described this in the words “optional and mandatory security review”. I say: You have to read the law completely. If an authority has a task, it shall fulfil this task, in accordance with the powers conferred on it for the management of tasks. That was the great innovation of the Security Police Act that defined tasks very clearly and gave powers. So if you have the task of protecting the constitutional bodies and their ability to act not only up-to-date but also preventively, you also have the task of getting the relevant information and not waiting for any officials to report to you and say: We may have a problem there, please do something! – This is the task of preventing the safeguarding of certain legal goods. What does § 55a tell us, when can a security check take place? – It may then be carried out in order to fulfil the task of preventing the protection of property in the case of ‘safety of legal exercise or [...] confidentiality of confidential information’. – That is the point on which the BAT committee is concerned. That is, the concrete accusation is that it has failed the police and thus your area of responsibility to collect and evaluate the relevant information. Another thing: § 55b clearly states that whenever it comes to functions in top-level organs, the Minister of the Interior himself is responsible for it as the supreme organ of the federal government. So you can't argue that you didn't have any information here. The following footnote is added: Yes, of course it would have been better for me and us, If Parliament's security department had made a corresponding request to the Ministry of the Interior – this has not been done, and this is bad that it has not been done. But I have to say a word on the subject: It corresponds manifestly to You've been in office for more than 12 months, always getting rid of anyone who wouldn't have done that right, who wouldn't have done it wrong, who wouldn't have been you. Now it is supposed to have been Parliament and Parliament officials who have failed to do something here. – This is legally wrong and politically blamable.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e17424	As next speaker, I would like to say: Mrs Eva Maria Holzleitner. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e17446	Madam President. Mrs. Federal Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! Dear Seers! Lootboxes are a hidden danger and a addiction potential in computer games. - We've heard a lot about it now. What are loot boxes? – I have had to explain this more recently, because it is partly really unknown: They are so-called booty boxes and a widely used game mechanics in computer games. Originally designed to give small incentives to players with cosmetic objects, the whole has developed into hidden gambling, which is specifically aimed at children and young people. Lootboxes can be purchased for sale, and their audio effects and visual effects are very similar to those you know from the casino, and these effects also activate the same area in the brain as those in the casino. There are different rules in different countries; for example, there is a mandatory disclosure in China of statistical opportunities and possible prices. Other solutions are the extension of the gambling ban for minors to computer games that include loot boxes – that implies that these games are only available with a release of 18 or more. For addicts there are issue limits in daily, weekly or monthly ratios. The difficulty in doing so is the burden of proof when minors carry out all these monetary transactions with a credit card from adults. This mixture of gambling and gaming really includes a very high addiction potential that you can't explore, and especially for children and young people the whole thing can become a cost trap. In several European countries, there is already a ban on these loot boxes in the area, and Austria too must really focus on this issue and make preventive and informative offers. It needs clearer advice, information for children and young people, but also prevention and education on the topic of lootboxing for adults and parents. It needs this clear marking, if not even a ban on this gambling. The Netherlands and Belgium have already proposed this, and Austria should start to act there. It may also be necessary to collect valid data which represent this addiction potential in general. We have tabled this request to the Family Committee and you, Mrs Minister, have already requested this request to act and also to work for an EU-wide regime. I am really pleased that there is a cross-group consensus here and that we are then continuing to address this issue in the Financial Committee, because this issue is simply a state-of-the-art and important for the protection of children and young people in Austria. Thank you.

The point is the protection of young people: Children do not know what a risk they are taking; Parents do not know what a risk their children face, and that is why we need to inculcate fully and urgently in the field of youth protection. Ladies and gentlemen have mentioned this: The Netherlands and Belgium have done this. I've seen on Sky News today that Britain is also dealing with the subject – it's just come up there. There you have many other problems, but even there you deal with lootboxes. The great unity that exists here in the house, as I hope, is not based on the fact that hardly anyone knows about this topic – that would be a bad statement – but I believe that it is a great concern to all in this House, since in terms of youth protection, there is something to do – a thanks also to colleague Holzleitner for this initiative. I believe that we are doing something on this issue with a common request across-the-board, is also an important signal that more people are aware of this and that parents are also involved in this issue. – In this sense: Thank you very much, and I'm sure we'll get it.

In the government programme – I have already mentioned it – there are very ambitious goals to be found again, unfortunately there is something missing in my view the strategy. So there are a lot of objectives inside, but little to be implemented. However, what the government has set in motion or what it is doing to get on the road is evaluation, and this is also a positive one. However, what is completely excluded, and this is very symptomatic, I believe, for our country and also for the governments of recent years, including for these, is that the vote with the federal states does not work again. In particular, the topic of transparency database will not be addressed by you either. We have a lot of opportunities to make it even more efficient by saving double-tracks. This is an issue that is being discussed far too little, especially in such an important area in which we need to invest as efficiently as possible, because we still have a lot ahead of us. We have talked about how much we still have to do in rural areas, how much further action is needed. In this context, I should like to remind you of a request from the last legislative period, a request from Mr Hafenecker and Mr Deimek on the subject. ‘Performing Internet for Hasendorf’. Hasendorf will probably tell you something else, a 193-inhabitant community. The motion for a resolution is about asking the Federal Minister to provide fast Internet in this municipality. It is still my knowledge that Hasendorf – and this is only a substitute for many rural regions – does not have a high-speed internet. I believe that we must work precisely to ensure that we get into the area as well. There are hundreds of such examples in rural areas in particular. I myself come from a small town with 50 inhabitants, we are far from Highspeedinternet. This is how the whole rural area is going, which, of course, is a very, very serious disadvantage for the local economy. Sepp Schellhorn can report how this is mostly in rural areas, in the hotel industry, if you tell the guests without fast internet we do not come to you. There are very large disadvantages, including for young entrepreneurs who want to settle in rural areas. We know that the Internet is the starting point that without the Internet today simply nothing goes, no matter if that's in the economic field, whether it's in education, wherever, even in the private sector, more and more people use streaming services instead of classic television, and so on. All these measures that we must put in place must be speeded up. I therefore call on the government to press the start button again and finally to really give full gas, otherwise it will not be with high speed on the Internet.

There are the programs access, backhaul, empty pipe and so on, you just have to get the money. I would just like to mention because some of the rural regions are talking about: In my home region, FTTH is currently being expanded, and the federal strategy is a very essential basis for this. The report is very good. It shows some potential for improvement in broadband targets, and that is a good thing. We need at least 100 Mbit/s and no low bandwidths, we need better coordination between the local authorities and much more. If I speak on this issue, I would like to point out a few things that I think are essential. We were recently on a study trip in Sweden. Sweden is considered to be a pioneer country in the field of digitalisation, not only in the software, but also in the digital infrastructure. What do the Sweden do differently? – The very decisive thing is once: You have to rely on the right technology. Broadband is transmitted via copper network, broadband is transmitted via coax network, broadband comes via satellite, broadband comes over the mobile phone, over mobile. Quite clear, and that's the big challenge: In the field of broadband expansion, we have to put high-performance internet on fiber optics. This has proved to be quite clear about the study trip. If we want high-performance Internet, we need the technology called fiber optics, because in principle we can transfer unlimited amounts of data. The second major issue is: We need to get away from the bandwidth targets, so not 30 mbits, but 100 mbits. We need an infrastructure target! If you realize that fiber optics is the only technology that can do this, then we need the infrastructure target. It is also important to stress that some people think that due to the new generation of mobile phones 5G, which also has high bandwidths, we are saving ourselves the fiber-optic expansion – I would like to say quite clearly: On the contrary! 5G transmitters need the fiber optic infrastructure, one due to the other. If we look into the future, then I have to say – and this is my great appeal – the only possible solution to move forward on this issue is that we have good cooperation between the local authorities. This starts at the European level. If we discuss some matters nationally, it is said that competition law at European level does not allow this because it is, of course, a market intervention. We therefore need measures at European level. We need a continuation of the programmes which expire at the end of 2020; BMVIT has already announced a new strategy. I can thank you for the good dialogue we have with the ministry. That's where we're on a very good way. And we need the countries, no question at all. Lower Austria, Upper Austria has established state infrastructure companies and we need to get into operational implementation. The Federation, ladies and gentlemen, will never operate on these fibre-optic networks, but the Federation will create the framework conditions and will provide the funding scene. I'm very concerned about the communities. I experience it all the time: There, where we have dedicated municipal councils, where we have committed mayors, there the fibre optics networks are built, because they know the situation on the spot best. They need support at national level, they need the federal funding scene, they need the European framework conditions, then, I am very convinced, we will make good progress on this issue. It needs a joint effort. I would like to thank the Court of Auditors once again for the very professional report, and I am looking forward to a good fiber optic future in Austria. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e19572	Madam President. High house! First of all, I would like to thank you very much for your intensive consideration of the Court of Auditors' reports. There are four reports on the agenda, which concern the transport sector, which we last discussed in the Court of Auditors Committee. I also agree to take a short look at the reports, because they are our reports. In the field of civil aviation security, we compared the Austro control with other European air navigation organisations and noted: The Austro Control is a productive air traffic control organization, but also a little more expensive. It has the task of ensuring the safe operation of air traffic. The essential quality feature is for example the number of delays, and this is where the Austro control is better than the European section. However, it is more expensive in the personnel costs – including in the air traffic controller’s hour – and is the eleventh place of European air traffic control organisations. The most important sources of revenue are the overflight charges and the arrival and departure fees. In 2015, there was a better result, the result was doubled due to a special effect – because the Ukrainian airspace was blocked. That is why the result and revenue were better. However, there is one but at Austro Control, which is the high share of social capital, the high provisions and settlements for pensions that represent two-thirds. These are, of course, sums that you have to earn and that will require a high level of liquidity in the future. The Austro Control was distributed in 1994 as you know. It employs more than 1,000 staff, including 336 air traffic controllers. The Court of Auditors has criticised the lack of a ownership strategy to reduce staff expenditure or to develop the design of salaries. We have found that the salaries of the active staff in the Austro Control were significantly higher than in the Federal Service. They have developed more dynamically since there was a collective agreement, and we are now not talking about the air traffic controllers, but rather the administrative staff, the technical specialists in the field of et cetera. There were performance premiums, there were rewards that we could not understand in part. The Court of Auditors therefore considers that measures should help to ensure that there is a more cost-effective payment. There is an opportunity for the third collective agreement to be negotiated; we also understand our report as a backwind for the negotiations. It is with the adaptation processes in relation to pension reforms. These were not withdrawn, in particular for staff subject to the old collective agreement. There is also a need to put in place cost-damaging measures. Legal measures have been excluded from the Committee, but measures such as 40-year settlement, early retirement payments, pension contributions et cetera would have to be put in place. As far as implementation is concerned, a demand process is currently taking place, and this will also be published in connection with the activity report for this year. On the subject of pensions, I come to the following report: Pensions provided by ÖBB staff. We have made a follow-up review of an audit report published by the Court of Auditors in 2015. I would like to stress that this report concerned those federal railway officials who were admitted until 1996 because the newer staff are subject to the ASVG from 1996 onwards and are not covered by this report. We have also made recommendations, and we have found that the failure to implement the recommendations has affected the ministry. These are proposals for amendments to the Federal Railway Pension Act. They did not come; the ÖBB implemented the recommendation addressed to them on the subject of organisational retirement schemes. As a result of this non-implementation, the savings potential has been reduced. The potential savings are becoming less and less because time goes on, of course, and they now amount to 560 million euros. The main criticism of the ÖBB-pensions – with the older railway officials – is the low retirement age, not the level of pensions. The average retirement age rose to 56 years, but is still well below the statutory retirement age. The age-related retirement age has risen to just over 60 years, but as a result of illness it was still at 54.39 years. At the time, we also recommended that the legislation for federal officials be brought together in the Federal Ministry of Public Service and Sport or in the Federal Chancellery. This recommendation has not yet been implemented. We believe that there are parallels and that this should be made central. We recognise that a few measures have been taken, such as no more organisational retirement schemes. The disease-related retirement changes were slightly declining, the age-related changes have increased slightly. Age part-time models are increasingly adopted. To the Federal Institute for Transport: This was a routine audit by the Court of Auditors, and we had to find a number of serious problems in this examination which had been there. There were two aspects: There is a duty for the air accidents safety investigation centre to publish reports of investigation. There is a time limit for publication, which was also not observed, and 36 investigations were discontinued at the beginning of 2016. There is, of course, also a legal and European-standard independence of the security investigation body. There were indications that these could or might have been interrupted by an unlawful instruction. The second issue was the purchase of external services from companies. These amounted to 24 million euros in the period from 2006 to 2016. There were procurement problems, there were not enough savings, and there were final payments of 348,000 euros, and the counter-performance was not understandable for us. We have forwarded our audit result to the prosecutors so that any criminal offences can be resolved. Measures have also been taken from the Ministry's side after our final meeting. We found that there was an overdose of the Bundesanstalt für Verkehr. The Head of Unit had practically the power to take up financial commitments indefinitely. Neither the Rules of Procedure nor the Ministry's revision regulations provided for any restrictions, and this allowed for a great margin of action. The Bundesanstalt für Verkehrs was dissolved with 31.7.2017. The safety investigation body is established as a subsidiary service, the technical on-road control has been at Asfinag since mid-June 2015, and we consider it necessary and appropriate that all problems be addressed and track tracking carried out. [[Abg. Lausch: (in the direction of Abg. Mr. Leichtfried, did you hear that anyway? Regress! Rehabilitation!]] As far as the broadband strategy is concerned, we have examined it, and I thank you for having found this report so intense and so great. I think this is a relevant issue. The National Council has also taken up two recommendations in the Telecommunication Act Novelle, namely the data on broadband provision, which must be made available there. With the expansion of broadband Internet in areas where there is no adequate supply, it was of course the goal of reducing the digital divide. In addition to the expansion, it is mainly about increasing the use of fast Internet. The government programme has ambitious objectives that reach up to gigabits by 2025. Countries have the opportunity to make top-up promotions to make connection promotions. We believe that it is important that coordination problems be resolved, that the transparency database is used. The funding – as already mentioned – is: 60 percent federal government, 40 percent contributions from the entrepreneurs concerned and also a case fee per case of 78 euros. To understand, this works as follows: If a passenger, a guest, has a problem with any transport institution, be it ship, train or aircraft, then he first has to contact the company concerned. If he does not continue with his complaint, the second instance is the passenger and passenger rights agency, which then takes care of this case, and there is a case fee of 78 euros to be paid. This case-by-case fee has also contributed, according to the report, to the fact that companies are trying to clarify the cases in the first instance as far as possible in order to save themselves this case-by-case fee. For statistics, perhaps a few other figures: In 2017, there were 3 870 written complaints and 2 504 procedures were actually taken up by the passenger and passenger rights agency. How did this spread to different companies? – The main part is for the air transport sector: 1 887 procedures in 2017, 578 procedures in the rail sector, only 38 in bus transport and a single procedure in maritime transport. In total, more than 1 million euros were spent on passengers. In 2018, the situation worsened, you think only of the departure halls this summer. Flights have been cancelled, many flights have been delayed. Therefore, it is not surprising that in the first half of 2018, from January to July, a total of 1,942 procedures were already opened. This is a plus of saying and writing 45 percent compared to 2017, so exorbitant much. It is also seen that this agency is, in fact, an important and essential institution for passenger and passenger rights, which takes care of the interests of customers. [[Intrusion of the Abg. The Fund shall draw its funds from the fee paid in the case of the reservation of a wish-mark. 60 percent of this transport safety contribution goes back to that federal country in which the desired mark has been applied for and reserved. The Fund must be used in accordance with the law for the following purposes: to promote measures to improve road safety, research in the field of road safety and road safety plans. The Court of Auditors noted that the Federal Ministry of Transport, Innovation and Technology has not implemented, in part and in part, the twelve revised recommendations of the previous report. In addition, the Court of Auditors noted that it would be useful to install part of the provisions of the Transport Safety Fund at low risk. In addition, a change in the law of the motor vehicle law would have to be addressed. The Ministry of Transport is to be given the opportunity not only to show them, but also to take appropriate sanctions in the event of the unlawful use of the land resources. With its projects, the Transport Safety Fund makes a significant contribution to raising road safety on Austria's roads. Transport safety campaigns can save lives. I am therefore pleased that the Federal Ministry of Transport is consistently implementing the Court's proposals. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e20426	Finally, we arrive at the 16th. Agenda item. We are going into the debate. I give the floor to the applicant, Mrs. Griss. Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2018-11-21-026-XXVI-NRSITZ-00049_d7e20453	Trust in justice is one of the basic prerequisites for peaceful living together. Justice is trusted if it is perceived as independent. Justice, these are the courts, and that's also the prosecutor's office. Since the Federal Constitutional Law Novelle 2008 the prosecutor's office has also been enshrined in the Constitution, as an organ of jurisdiction. This means that we have bodies of jurisdiction, which are not only bound by directions – which is already an anomaly – but which are still subject to a member of the government, namely the Minister of Justice. We now have a board of instructions, but this is half a solution, because the head of direction is still the Federal Minister for Justice. This is a situation that weakens confidence in justice. We have seen this very clearly in the BAT-Causa, in which the suspicion arose that there is something in the government and that the prosecution cannot act as independently as it should. This demand to stop having the Minister of Justice as the leader, but to use an independent state attorney, namely by Parliament, has been raised for decades. It is also standard in Western European democracies. The former Minister of Justice Brandstetter also called for this – then he became Minister of Justice, and that demand was no longer up-to-date. Therefore, I believe that we should now tackle this, because it does not only violate the separation of violence, it has also become even more critical because of the large number of divisions that the public prosecutor now expresses. Thus, prosecutors also have a more or less judicial function. I therefore believe that it is necessary that it is time to free the Minister of Justice from this task to release it from the government and to use an independent state attorney for twelve years, similar to the President of the Court of Auditors, without possibility of being re-appointed. This would help to ensure that justice is actually retrusted. Thank you.
